     220 END

4-34 Programming
                              ARRAYDEF

ARRAYDEF

        Array Definition
                      Syntax

Description                   The ARRAYDEF command allows you to create user-defined arrays
                              of the type REAL, INTEGER, or BYTE. The number of elements in
                              the array is limited only by the amount of free user memory in the
                              module. In array operations, arrays of different types (such as REAL,
                              INTEGER, or BYTE) cannot be operated on unless they are accessed
                              element by element. For example a REAL array defined by the
                              command in line 10 below, then executed with line 20, is an invalid
                              combination.

                                       10 OUTPUT 718;"ARRAYDEF REAL,TST,GOl;"
                                      20 OUTPUT 718;"MOV TRA,TST;"

                              This is because TST is a REAL array and TRA is an integer array. A
                              valid command combination to access an array element by element is
                              as follows:

                                       10 OUTPUT 718;"ARRAYDEF REAL,TST,GOl;"
                                      20 OUTPUT 718; "MOV TRA[l],TST[l];"

                              Restrictions: Subject to available memory. The following commands
                              consume memory: ONEOS, ARRAYDEF, TRDEF, KEYDEF, LCLVAR,
                              VARDEF REPEAT/UNTIL, and IF/THEN.

Query Response                For array data transfer to and from an external controller, only M
                              format is supported. A, B, I, and P block-data field formats are not
                              currently supported.

                              Programming 4-35
ARRMDEF

Program Example

lo- ! The following example shows the use of the ARRAYDEF command. The

20 ! example uses an input signal of 300 MHz such as the CAL OUTPUT and

30 ! looks at its harmonics.

40 !

50 ASSIGN QSa TO 718                   ! Assign I/O path to address 718.

60 !

70 OUTPUT QSa;"ARRAYDEF REAL,FREQS,lO;"; ! Create a lo-element REAL array.

80 OUTPUT OSa ;"ARRAYDEF REAL,AMPLS,lO;"; ! Create another REAL array.

90 OUTPUT OSa ;"VARDEF X,l;VARDEF Y,600;"; ! Create, initialize two variables.

100 OUTPUT @Sa ;"IP;FA IOOMHZ;";       ! Preset; set start frequency.

110 OUTPUT @Sa ;"FB 2900MHZ;TS;";      ! Set stop frequency; take sweep.

120 OUTPUT OSa ;"MKPK HI;";            ! Marker to peak of highest signal.

130 !

140 OUTPUT OSa*"REPEAT*"*              ! Begin loop.

150 OUTPUT QSaj" MOV ;REQS[XI,MKF;";   ! Put marker frequency into Xth

160 ! element of FREQS array.

170 OUTPUT (PSa;" MOV AMPLSCXI,MKA;";  ! Put marker amplitude into Xth

180 ! element of AMPLS array.

190 OUTPUT QSa;" MKPK NH;";            ! Marker to next highest peak.

200 OUTPUT (PSa;" ADD X,X,1;";         ! Increment array pointer.

210 OUTPUT @Sa;"UNTIL X,GT,lO;";       ! Loop until array pointer > 10.

220 !

230 OUTPUT OSa;"CLRDSP;";              ! Clear analyzer display.

240 OUTPUT (OSa;"PA 110,650;";               ! Move pen to starting point.
                                       AMPLITUDE(dBm)%;";
250 ouwm (OSa;"TEXT %FREQUENCY(Hz)

                                       ! Write titles.

260 OUTPUT OSa; "MOV X,1;";            ! Initialize array pointer.

270 OUTPUT @Sa8*"PA 100,Y*9"*2         ! Pen to first column.

280 !

290 OUTPUT QSa*9"REPEAT*,"*,           ! Begin loop.

300 OUTPUT OSa;" DSPLY FREQSCXl,l0,3;"; ! Display Xth frequency.

310 OUTPUT (PSa;" TEXT %        X;";   ! Space over to next column.

320 OUTPUT QSa;" DSPLY AMPLS[XI,l0,3;"; ! Display Xth amplitude.

330 OUTPUT (DSa;" SUE3 Y,Y,30;";       ! Decrement pen location.

340 OUTPUT (DSa;" PA 100,Y;";          ! Move pen to new location

350 OUTPUT OSa;" ADD X,X,1;";          ! Increment array pointer.

360 OUTPUT BSa;"UNTIL X,GT,lO;";       ! Loop until array pointer > 10.

370

380 ASSIGN OSa TO * ! Close I/O path.

390 END

4-36 Programming
                                                                                          AUTOEXEC

AUTOEXEC

  Automatic Execution
                      Syntax

Description                   Turns off or on the automatic function as defined with the
                              AUTOFUNC command.

                              Prerequisite Command: AUTOFUNC

                              Related Commands: AUTOSAVE, CLRSCHED

Program Example

10 ! The following example shows the use of the AUTOEXEC command.

20 !

30 ASSIGN QSa TO 718          ! Assign I/O path to address 718.

40 !

50 Autoexec,status=999        ! Initialize status variable to

60                            ! ELSE case value.

70 OUTPUT QSa;"AUTOEXEC ON;"; ! Turn AUTOEXEC function on.

80 OUTPUT OSa;"AUTOEXEC?;"; ! Query its status.

90 ENTER OSa;Autoexec-status ! Get the status from the analyzer.

100 GOSUR Check-status

110 !

120 OUTPUT OSa;"AUTOEXEC OFF;"; ! Turn AUTOEXEC function off.

130 OUTPUT QSa;"AUTOEXEC?;"; ! Query its status.

140 ENTER OSa;Autoexec,status ! Get the status from the analyzer.

150 GOSUB Check-status

160 STOP

170 !

180 Check-status:             ! Subroutine to display the status of

190                           ! the AUTOEXEC function.

200 SELECT Autoexec,status

210 CASE 0

220      PRINT "The AUTOEXEC function is OFF."

230 CASE 1

240      PRINT "The AUTOEXEC function is ON."

250 CASE ELSE

260 ! This condition does not exist, however it is a good programming

270 ! practice to include an ELSE case for unexpected situations.

280      PRINT "The AUTOEXEC function returned an unknown status value."

290 END SELECT

300 RETURN                    ! From Check-status subroutine.

310 END

                                                        Programming 4-37
AUTOFUNC                      AUTOFUNC

   Automatic Function
                      Syntax

                                                       /

                                        rstop t i m e - \

Description                   This command specifies the operation for automatic execution. Use
                              the AUTOEXEC or AUTOSAVE function to turn execution on or off.
                              When using LMTST as a trigger, the start and stop times must be valid
                              dates (for example, 000000000000 or 999999999999 are not valid.)
                              The specific operation may be one of the following:

                              l Store trace A data.
                              l Store trace B data.
                              n Execute a user-defined function.

                              when one of the following conditions occur:

                              n A specified time period elapses.
                              n The end of the sweep occurs.
                              n Current trace data exceeds limit-line values.

4-38 Programming
Parameters                                                                          AUTOFUNC

            1. There can be from one to seven processes scheduled.
            2. TRA ( TRB 1 DLP (TRA, TRB for autosaving traces; DLP:: = name of

                user-defined function.)
            3. Start-time format: MMDDYYHHMMSS or DDMMYYHHMMSS

               (depending on datemode format)
            4. Stop-time: MMDDYYHHMMSS or DDMMYYHHMMSS (depending on

                datemode format)
            5. EOS / LMTST ( INTVL selects criteria to perform autosave or to

               execute a DLP.
                a. EOS occurs at the end of a sweep.
               b. LMTST occurs at the end of a sweep after a limit test failure.
                c. IN'I'VL occurs after the end of a sweep, when the first

                   designated time interval is reached. The format for an interval
                   entry is HHMMSS.
            6. INTVL occurs at the interval time if INTVL is selected.

            Restrictions: Subject to available memory. The following commands
            consume memory: ONEOS, ARRAYDEF, TRDEF, KEYDEF, LCLVAR,
            VARDEF REPEAT/UNTIL, and IF/THEN.

            Prerequisite Commands: FUNCDEF, CONTS, TITLE, SLRW, and
            limit-line commands.

            Related Commands: CLRDSP, TITLE, ABORT, RETURN, PAUSE,
            ERASE, DISPOSE.

Program Example

  10 ! The following example shows the use of the AUTOFUNC command.
  20 !
  30 ASSIGN cOSa TO 718 ! Assign I/O path to address 718.
  40 !
  50 OUTPUT @Sa; "IP; `I ; ! Instrument preset.
  60 ! Create three items for the AUTOEXEC Schedule.
  70 OUTPUT QSa;"AUTOFUNC 1 ,B0X,010189000000,010589000000 EOS;";
  80 OUTPUT OSa;"AUTOFUNC 2 ,CHK,S1G,123188235500,123188235900 INTVL 000030;";
 90 OUTPUT OSa;"AUTOFUNC 3 TRA,122488090000,122488125900 LMTST;";
  100 PRINT "Press CLCL) [MODULE] AUTOEXEC MENU to see the new schedule."
  110 DISP "Press [CONTINUE] on the computer when ready"
  120 PAUSE
  130 !
  140 ! Remove one item from the schedule.
  150 OUTPUT OSa;"IP;CLRSCHED 2;";
  160 PRINT "Press (LCL) [MODULE] {AUTOEXEC MENU) to see the updated schedule."
  170 DISP "Press [CONTINUE] on the computer when ready"
  180 PAUSE
  190 I
 200 ! Clear the entire schedule.
 210 OUTPUT QSa;"IP;CLRSCHED ALL;";
 220 PRINT "Press (LCL) [MODULE] (AUTOEXEC MENU) to see the cleared schedule."
 230 DISP 'I"
 240 !
 250 ASSIGN QSa TO *
 260 END

            Programming 4-39
AUTOSAVE

Automatically Save Traces
                      Syntax

Description       When set to ON, this command activates the automatic saving of
                  traces. The data to be automatically saved must be identified in the
                  AUTOFUNC command.

                  Prerequisite Commands: AUTOFUNC and TITLE.

                  Restrictions: A maximum of one trace per second and subject to
                  available memory. The following commands consume memory:
                  ONEOS, ARRAYDEF, TRDEF, KEYDEF, LCLVAR, VARDEF
                  REPEAT/UNTIL, and IF/THEN.

4-40 Programming
                                                                                       AVG

AVG

     Average                               ,- d e s t i n a t i o n - . ,
      Syntax

                                               roverag. r a t i o - \
                                                        user-defined

                                           \ vorioble

                                                Lpredefined J
                                                             variable

                                           L p r e d e f ined 2
                                                        function

                                           Lr t r a c e                             i
                                                                           element

     Description      The data in the source and destination are averaged, then stored in
                      the destination. The following averaging algorithm is used:

                          DESTnew = ((RATIO - 1) x DESTold + SOURCE)/RATIO

     Parameter Range  Average Ratio:

                        Minimum: -32,767
                        0 is not allowed
                        Maximum: + 32,767

                                                                                       Programming 4-41
AVG

     Program Example

     10 ! The following example shows the use of the AVG command.

     20 !

     30 ASSIGN OSa TO 718                   ! Assign I/O path to address 7 .8.

     40 !

442 Programming
                                                             CARDLOAD

CARDLOAD

Copy Data From Memory Card to Module Memory

Syntax                 ,-file n a m e -,

             CARDLOAD  del i m i t e r       de i m i t e r

Description  Copy the specified data from the memory card to the module
             battery-backed memory. The filename label (identifier) should follow
             this format: 1 to 9 ASCII characters followed by a period, then
             followed by one of these three-letter suffixes: DLP or LMT. Valid
             ASCII characters that may be selected are: A through Z, 0 through 9,
             and _ (underscore).

             A limit line may be created with the extension .lim appended to
             the filename. The extension LMT must be used when using the
             CARDLOAD and CARDSTORE commands with mass memory module
             firmware revisions before 950829. For later firmware, either extension
             is allowed.

             The following statement copies the DLP "ABCDEFGHI" from the
             memory card to the module:

                 CARDLOAD %ABCDEFGHI.DLP%;

                                             Programming 443
CARDLOAD                 DLPs, traces, and limit lines stored on memory cards using a
                         mass-memory module with firmware datecode 910116 or later cannot
      Program Example    be read into a module with an earlier firmware datecode (for example
                   Note  890524). DLFs, traces, and limit lines stored on cards using modules
                         with firmware datecode 890524 or earlier can be read into a module
                         with firmware datecode 910116 or later.

10 ! The following example shows the use of the CARDLOAD command. The

20 ! example creates a DLP (downloadable program) and stores it in memory.

30 ! It is then copied to the memory card, if available.

40 !

50 ASSIGN QISa TO 718                  ! Assign I/O path to address 718.

60 !

70 OUTPUT OSa;"FUNCDEF BOX,@;";        ! Logical start of function 'BOX'.

80 OUTPUT OSa;" IP;";                  ! Instrument preset.

90 OUTPUT (PSa;" CLRDSP;";             ! Clear the analyzer screen.

100 OUTPUT (OSa;" PU;PA 300,300;";     ! Move pen to starting point.

110 OUTPUT (PSa;" PD;PR 240,O;";       !\

120 OUTPUT OSa;" PR 0,240;";           ! \ Draw

130 OUTPUT (OSa;" PR -240,O;";         ! / rectangle.

140 OUTPUT Wa;" PR 0,-240;";           !/

150 OUTPUT OSa;"O;";                   ! Logical end of function.

160 !

170 OUTPUT OSa;"CARDSTORE %BOX.DLP%;"; ! Copy DLP to memory card.

180 !

190 ! The DLP now appears on the catalog of the memory card available in

200 ! the module utility menu.

210 !

220 OUTPUT OSa;"DISPOSE BOX;";         ! Remove the DLP 'BOX' from

230                                    ! the module memory. It is

240                                    ! still on the card!!

250 !

260 OUTPUT OSa;"CARDLOAD %BOX.DLP%;"; ! Copy DLP from card to module

270                                    ! memory. Now it's in both places.

280 !

290 ASSIGN OSa TO * ! Close I/O path.

300 END

4-44 Programming
                              CARDSTORE

CARDSTORE

Copy Data to a Memory Card
                      Syntax

Description  Copy the specified data from the module memory to a RAM card. The
    Note     filename should follow this format: 1 to 9 ASCII characters followed
             by a period, then followed by one of these three-letter suffixes: DLP
             or LMT. Valid ASCII characters that may be selected are: A through Z
             (upper-case only), 0 through 9, and _ (underscore). RAM cards use the
             Logical Interchange Format (LIF) and therefore accept only 9 of the
             16 characters used to title data.

             The Iirst 9 characters must be unique to avoid writing over an existing
             file. If a file name is longer than 9 characters or if lower-case letters
             are used, the file name will be converted, if possible, to an LIF
             compatible file name using only the first 9 characters.

Note         A limit line may be created with the extension Jim appended to
             the filename. The extension .LMT must be used when using the
             CARDLOAD and CARDSTORE commands with mass memory module
             firmware revisions before 950829. For later firmware, either extension
             is allowed.

             DLPs, traces, and limit lines stored on memory cards using a mass-
             memory module with firmware datecode 910116 or later cannot be
             read into a module with an earlier firmware datecode (for example
             890524). DLPs, traces, and limit lines stored on cards using modules
             with firmware datecode 890524 or earlier can be read into a module
             with firmware datecode 910116 or later.

                              Programming 4-45
CARDSTORE

Program Example

10 ! The following example shows the use of the CARDSTORE command. The

20 ! example creates a DLP (downloadable program) and stores it in memory.

30 ! It is then copied to the memory card, if available.

40 !

50 ASSIGN OSa TO 718               ! Assign I/O path to address 718.

60 !

70 OUTPUT QSa;"FUNCDEF BOX,(O;";   ! Logical start of function 'BOX'.

80 OUTPUT QSa;" IP;";              ! Instrument preset.

90 OUTPUT OSa;" CLRDSP;";          ! Clear the analyzer screen.

100 OUTPUT OSa;" PU;PA 300,300;";  ! Move pen to starting point.

110 OUTPUT (DSa;" PD;PR 240,O;";   !\

120 OUTPUT (DSa;" PR 0,240;";      ! \ Draw

130 OUTPUT &?a;" PR -240,O;";      ! / rectangle.

140 OUTPUT @Sa;" PR 0,-240;";      !/

150 OUTPUT QSa;'W;";               ! Logical end of function.

160 !

170 OUTPUT QSa;"CARDSTORE #BOX.DLP%;"; ! Copy DLP to memory card.

180 ASSIGN QISa TO *               ! Close I/O path.

190 END

446 Programming
                              CATALOG

CATALOG

Directory Listing over HPIB
                      Syntax

Description     The CATALOG command returns a directory listing of the current
   Note         mass storage device over the HPIB interface. The mass storage device
                should be specified using MSDEV prior to executing the CATALOG
                command.

                Related command: MSDEV

                The listing is in the form of one string terminated by a linefeed with
                EOI. The string is divided into two substrings by a linefeed character.
                The first substring contains the actual listing and the second substring
                contains the amount of free memory in bytes. Refer to the Query
                Response figure and Pile Information Parameters table for more
                information.

                The CATALOG command is only available in firmware revision
                datecodes of 910116 and later.

Query Response

                                                                                                                                                                                         Ilf21a

                Where:
                  filename up to 16 characters (up to 9 if MSDEV is CARD)
                  filelength up to 6 characters
                  timestamp 12 characters
                  title up to 32 characters

                * These paths only available when MSDEV is MEM.
                t This path only available when MSDEV is CARD.

                                                                                                Programming 4-47
CATALOG

File Information Parameters

                              File Information                    Description
                  filename.LIM
                  filename.VAR                  This file contains a limit line

                                                This file is a variable defined using the
                                                VARDEF, or TRDEF, or ARRAYDEF
                                                command.

                                                It is only available when MSDEV is set
                                                to MEM.

                  f ilename.DLP                 If MSDEV is set to MEM this is the
                                                downloadable program defined using the
                                                FUNCDEF command.

                  filename.timestamp.TRC        This file is a trace. It can be recalled
                                                by way of the front panel or by using
                                                filename.timestamp asan argument
                                                to MODRCIX when MSDEV is set
                                                to MEM

                  filename.TRC :title:timestamp: This trace file is storedin the
                                                               memory card. When the file is copied
                                                               to the module memory, it will be
                                                               stored under filename title
                                                               with the corresponding t imest amp.

Program Examde 1  The following example simply queries the module memory for its
                  catalog and prints the string returned from the module. Example 2
                  shows how to query the catalog and parse the output string.

10 ASSIGN @Sa TO 718

20 DIM Cat$[lOOOOl                        ! Dimension string arbitrarily large.

30 DIM Bytes,avail$[20]                   ! Must accommodate "BYTES FREE XXXXXX<lf>".

40 OUTPUT @a-"MSDEV MEM." ! Specify MSDEV to be cataloged.

60 OUTPUT @SaI"CATALOG?;:' ! Query module for directory listing.

70 ENTER @Sa USING "#,K";Cat$,Bytes,avail$

                                          ! Data up to the first <If>

80                                        ! will go into Cat$; data up

90                                        ! to <If-EOI> will go into

100                                       ! Bytes,avail$.

110 PRINT Cat$

120 PRINT Bytes-avail$

130 ASSIGN QSa TO *

140 END

Program Example 2  The following example parses the output of the CATALOG command.
                   The program displays softkeys enabling the user to catalog the entire
                   mass storage device or only certain file types, such as DLPs, limit lines,
                   or traces. Variables can be cataloged only if the mass storage device is
                   MEM; variables cannot be stored to the memory card.

                   This example is written for HP BASIC 2.1 or greater and runs on an
                   HP Series 200/300 Controller.

10 !The following example shows the use of the CATALOG command. The example

20 !catalogs the current mass storage device (CARD or MEM) and parses the

30 !returned string into a holding array. Softkeys are then enabled to

40 !allow the user to select which part of the catalog to display.

50 !

60 INTEGERLast,match,Entry-name,Entry,length,Entry-type,Entry

70 INTEGERSpace,loc,Dot,loc,Two,dots

80 DIM String_parsed$[75],String$C751,Rev_str$C75l,Bytes-avail$ CiOl

90 DIM Mass,stg,dev$[ll,Temp$C751

100 DIM Ent$(l:3,1:500)[75],Cat$[5000]

110 !

120 ASSIGN OSa TO 718                        ! Spectrum analyzer HP-IB address

130 DISP "Loading user defined memory."

140 OUTPUT QSa*"MSDEV?*"

150 ENTER @Sa;Mass&dev$

160 IF Mass,stg,dev$="M" THEN PRINT "Mass Storage Device is MEMORY"

170 IF Mass-stg,dev$=" Cl' THEN PRINT "Mass Storage Device is CARD"

180 !

190 OUTPUT QSa*"CATALOG?*"

200 ENTER @Sa USING "#,N';Cat$,Bytes-avail$

210 PRINT Bytes,avail$;" (of available user memory)"

220 !

230 Entry=0

240 Entry,name=FNWhich,pos("NAME")

250 Entry,length=FNWhich_pos("LENGTH")

260 Entry,type=FNWhich,pos("TYPE")

270 REPEAT

280 Entry=Entry+l

290 CALL Universal-parse("," ,Cat$,String-parsed$,Last_match)

300 IF LEN(String,parsed$)=O THEN No-entries

310 Dot-loc=POS(String,parsed$,".")

320 Two-dots=POS(String,parsed$[Dot,loc+ll,".")

330 IF Two-dots THEN                         ! (it must be a trace in MEM)

                                                                     Programming 4-49
CATALOG

340                  Dot,loc=Two,dots+Dot,loc

350 END IF

360 Space-loc=POS(String_parsed$," ">

370 WHILE Space,loc<Dot-lot                         ! Ignore spaces which are

380                  Temp$=String,parsed$CSpace,loc+l] ! part of LIMIT LINE title.

390                  Space,loc=Space,loc+POS(Temp$," ">

400 END WHILE

410 !

420 String$=String-parsed$[l,Dot,loc-11

430 WHILE NUM(String$[l,l1)<32                      ! Strip any special characters from

440                  String$=String$[21                  ! system-generated dlp names.

450 END WHILE

460 Ent$(Entry,name,Entry)=String$

470               !

480 SELECT Mass-stg-dev$                                 !Allow for differences in string

490 CASE "M"                                             !of the Memory Card and Module Memory

500                  Ent$(Entry_length,Entry)=String,parsed$[Space-loc]

510 CASE "C"

520                  Rev-str$=REV$(String,parsed$)

530                  Ent$(Entry,length,Entry)=TRIM$(REV$(Rev,str$[l,POS(Rev-str$," ">I>>

540 END SELECT

550      !

560 Ent$(Entry_type,Entry)=TRIM$(String,parsed$[Dot-loc+l;4])

570 UNTIL Last-match

580 !

590 LOOP                                                 ! Display softkeys until Exit key

                                                         ! is pressed.

600 ON KEY 1 LABEL "DLP" GOSUB List-dlps

610 ON KEY 2 LABEL "All" GOSUB List-all

620 ON KEY 4 LABEL "Exit" RECOVER Exit-cat

630 ON KEY 3 LABEL "Lim Line" GOSUB List-limit

640 IF Mass,stg-dev$="M" THEN ON KEY 6 LABEL "Variable" GOSUB List,vars

650 !Variable key is only displayed for a catalog of MEM. Variables

660 !cannot be stored on the card.

670 ON KEY 8 LABEL "Trace" GOSUB List-traces

680 DISP "Select list mode."

690 END LOOP

700 No,entries:PRINT "No Catalog Entries ! !"

710 Exit,cat:ASSIGN QSa TO *

720 DISP "PROGRAM ENDED"

730 STOP
740 I-----------------------------------------------------------------l

750 List-dlps:Display-cat("DLP",Ent$(*),Entry)
760 RETURN
770 List-all:Display-cat("ALL",Ent$(*),Entry)
780 RETURN
790 List,vars:Display,cat("VAR",Ent$(*),Entry)
800 RETURN
810 List-traces:Display-cat("TRC",Ent$(*),Entry)
820 RETURN
830 List,limit:Display-cat("LIM",Ent$(*),Entry)
840 RETURN
850 END

4-50 Programming
                                                                           CATALOG

870 SUBUniversal,parse(Delimiter$,In_n_out$,String-parsed$,IN~GER Last-match)

880 !A generic parsing routine.

890 Universal-parse: !

900 INTEGERDelim-loc,Len-delim

910 Len,delim=LEN(Delimiter$)

920 Delim,loc=POS(In,n-out$,Delimiter$)

930 IF Delim,loc=O THEN

940     String-parsed$=In,n,out$

950     Last-match=1

960 ELSE

970     String,parsed$=TRIM$(In_n,out$[l,Delim-lot-11)

980     In-n,out$=TRIM$(In,n,out$[Delim-loc+Len-deliml)

990     Last-match=0

1000 END IF

1010 SUBEND                                   ! <Universal-parse>
1020 !----___----____-_--------------------------------------------------

1030 SUB Display,cat(Class$,Ent$(*),INTEGER Num,entries)

1040 !Displays the previously parsed catalog retrieved from the 85620A+.

1050 Display-cat:!

1060 INTEGEREntry,name,Entry,length,Entry,type,Entry

1070 REAL Total,length,Total-entries

1080 !

1090 Entry,name=FNWhich-pos("NAME")

1100 Entry,length=FNWhich,pos("LENGTH")

1110 Entry,type=FNWhich-pos("TYPE")

1120 Total-length=0

1130 Total-entries=0

1140 !

1150 1m:IMAGE 12A,2X,20A,2X,8D

1160 Im2:IMAGE 12A,2X,20A,2X,l5A

1170 DISP

1180 OUTPUTKBD;CHR$(255)t"K";                 ! Clear screen
                                       ,I II                       II ,I Byte total"
1190  PRINT  USING  Im2;" Type                     Name
1200  PRINT  USING                     It;,, --------------------  +I --em -- --m-w--- 11
                    Im2;" --__-------

1210 !

1220 FOR Entry=1 TO Num,entries

1230    IF Class$=Ent$(Entry,type,Entry) OR Class$="ALL" THEN

1240         SELECT Ent$(Entry,type,Entry)

1250       CASE "DLP"

1260         String$="DLP (Func) "

1270         CASE "VAR"

1280         String$="Variable '

1290       CASE "TRC"

1300         String$="Trace '

1310       CASE "LIM"

1320         String$="Limit Line '

1330       END SELECT

1340       PRINT USING Im;String$,Ent$(Entry,name,Entry),

                    VAL(Ent$(Entry,length,Entry))

1350       GOSUB Totals

1360    END IF

1370 NEXT Entry

1380 PRINT

1390 PRINT "TOTALS. ";VAL$(Total,length);" bytes."

1400 PRINT fl       ' ' ;VAL$(Total,entries);" entries."

                                                                   Programming 4-51
CATALOG

1410 SUBEXIT
1420 !

1430 Totals: !

1440 Total-entries=Total-entries+1
1450 Total,length=Total_length+VAL(Ent$(Entry-length,~try))

1460 RETURN

1470 !

1480 SUBEND
1490 !---------------------------------------------------------------1

1500 DEF FNWhich,pos(Class$)

1510 !Determines where information is placed in the array. Allows

1520 !for re-arranging of the array.

1530 Which,pos: !

1540 SELECT Class$
1550 CASE "NAME"

1560              RETURN 1

1570 CASE "LENGTH"

1580              RETURN 2

1590 CASE "TYPE"

1600              RETURN 3

1610 CASE ELSE

1620              RETURN0

1630 END SELECT

1640 FNEND

4-52 Programming

CLRDSP                                  (CLRDSP

             Clear Display
                      Syntax

     Description This command erases spectrum-analyzer display annotation.

                           Related Commands: DSPLY, OR, TEXT, PA, PR, PD, and PU

Program Example

10 ! The following example shows the use of the CLRDSP command.

20 !

30 ASSIGN (DSa TO 718                            ! Assign I/O path to address 718.

40 !

50 OUTPUT OSa;"CLRDSP;";                         ! Clears the analyzer display.

60 !

70 OUTPUT QSa;"VARDEF LOG-TEN,O;";               ! \ Initialize variables.

80 OUTPUT OSa;"VARDEF YVAL,700;";                !/

90 !

100 FOR I=100 TO 1000 STEP 100                   ! Begin loop.

110   OUTPUT OSa;"LOG LOG,TEN,"&VAL$(I>&",l;";

                                                 !Take the LOG and

120 ! multiply by scaling factor of 1.

130   OUTPUT OSa;"PU;PA 20,YVAL;";               ! Position "pen" for text.

140   OUTPUT (OSa;"TEXT %The LOG of %;'I; ! Write text.

150   OUTPUT QSa;"DSPLY "&VAL$(I)&",6,3;";

                                                 ! Write a value.

160   OUTPUT QSa;"TEXT % = 1;";                  ! Write text.

170   OUTPUT QSa;"DSPLY LOG_TEN,5,3;";           ! Write a value.

180   OUTPUT @Sa;'%JB YVAL,YVAL,50;";            ! Calculate new "pen" position.

190 NEXT I                                       ! End loop.

200 !                                            ! Close I/O path.
210 ASSIGN OSa TO *
220 END

CLRSCHED

Clear Autosave/Autoexec Schedule Buffer
                      Syntax

     Description  Clears the Autosave/Autoexec Schedule of all or individually specified
                  contents. Clear all schedule contents with the ALL command or
                  selectively by specifying which number (from one to seven) to clear.

Program Example

10 ! The following example shows the use of the CLRSCHED command.

20 !

30 ASSIGN 9Sa TO 718 ! Assign I/O path to address 718.

40 !

50 OUTPUT OSa*"IP*"- ! Instrument preset.

60 ! Create tkee'iiems for the AUTOEXEC Schedule.

70 OUTPUT QSa;"AUTOFUNC 1 ,B0X,010189000000,010589000000 EOS;";

80 OUTPUT OSa;"AUTOFUNC 2 ,CHK~S1G,123188235500,123188235900 INTVL 000030;";

90 OUTPUT OSa;"AUTOFUNC 3 TRA,122488090000,122488125900 LMTST;";

100 PRINT "Press (LCL) [MODULE] (AUTOEXEC MENU) to see the new schedule."

110 DISP "Press [CIJNTIIVUEI on the computer when ready"

120 PAUSE

130 !

140                   ! Remove one item from the schedule.

150 OUTPUT QSa;"IP;CLRSCHED 2;";

160 PRINT "Press (LCL) [MODULE] (AUTOEXEC MENU) to see the updated schedule."

170 DISP "Press [CONTINUE1 on the computer when ready"

180 PAUSE

190 !

200                   ! Clear the entire schedule.

210 OUTPUT 9Sa;"IP;CLRSCHED ALL;";

220 PRINT "Press (LCL) [MODULE] (AUTOEXEC MENU) to see the cleared schedule."

230 DISP "'I

240 !

250 ASSIGN cPSa TO *

260 END

CNTLA

Auxiliary Control Line A
                      Syntax

Description                   The CNTLA command* sets control line A of the auxiliary interface
                              high or low.

                              Related commands: CNTLB* , CNTLC*, CNTLD* , CNTLI*, and
                              OUTPUT*

                              Refer to "Specifications and Characteristics" in Chapter 1, Installation,
                              for a detailed description of the auxiliary interface.

Preset State On

Program Example

10 !The following example shows a use of the CNTLA command.

20 !

30 ASSIGN (BSa TO 718                   !Assign I/O path to address 718.

40 !

50 OUTPUT QSa;"VARDEF S,CNTLA,O;"       !CNTLA STATE

60 OUTPUT OSa;"FUNCDEF AUX,CLR,A,O;";

70 OUTPUT cDSa;" CNTLA 0;";             !SET LINE A TO 0

80 OUTPUT OSa;" MOV S,CNTLA,O;";        !UPDATE CNTLA IMAGE

90 OUTPUT (DSa;"Q;"

100 OUTPUT QSa;"FUNCDEF AUX-SET-A,@;";

110 OUTPUT OSa; I' CNTLA 1;";           !SET LINE A TO 1

120 OUTPUT QISa;" MOVE S,CNTLA,l;";     !UPDATE CNTLA IMAGE

130 OUTPUT OSa-"(9*"

140 OUTPUT QSai"AbX-CLR-A;"             !CLEAR LINE A

150 OUTPUT OSa;"S-CNTLA?;"              !GET LINE A STATUS

160 ENTER (OSa;A$

170 END

                              * These auxiliary interface connector commands can only be
                                  accessed remotely by way of the HPIB and DLPs and are only
                                  available with 85620A with serial prefix 3143A or higher and
                                  firmware 910116 and later date codes.

                                                             Programming 4-59
CTRLHPIB

            Control HP-IB
                      Syntax

Description                   This command takes control of the HP-IB. If a controller is active and
                              detected on the bus, then the command is not executed and an error
                              results. If none is detected, the spectrum analyzer assumes control by
                              asserting the remote-enable (REN) line. This command should precede
                              the related commands RELHPIB, ENTER, and OUTPUT.

4-60 Programming
                                                                   CTRLHPIB

Program Example

10 ! The following example shows how to use the CTRLHPIB command to have

20 ! the analyzer send data to another HP-IB device. The instructions

30 ! within the FUNCDEF '6' delimiters form a structure called a

40 ! DLP (downloadable program).

50 !

60 ! NOTE: A printer (HP-IB address 01) needs to be connected.

70 !

80 ASSIGN (OSa TO 718                   ! Assign I/O path to address 718.

90 !

100 OUTPUT OSa;"FUNCDEF E,XMPLE,Q;"; ! Logical start of the DLP.

110 OUTPUT QSa;" CTRLHPIB;";            ! Tells analyzer to take control

120                                     ! of the bus:

130 OUTPUT (PSa " MKN;";                ! Turn on a marker.

140 OUTPUT (BSa ' OUTPUT l,KC,'THIS IS AN EXAMPLE OF THE ANALYZER

SENDING'., ".,

150 OUTPUT QSa ' OUTPUT l,KC,'DATA TO ANOTHER HPIB DEVICE';";

160 OUTPUT @Sa II OUTPUT l,KC,";";

170 OUTPUT @Sa " OUTPUT l,K,'MARKER FREQUENCY = ';";

180 OUTPUT (BSa;" OUTPUT l,K,MKF;";

190 OUTPUT @Sa;" OUTPUT l,KC,' Hz';"*,

200 OUTPUT (PSa;" OUTPUT l,KC,";";

210 OUTPUT QSa;" RELHPIB;";             ! Release HP-IB control.

220 OUTPUT (PSa.8"(D,*"                 ! Logical end of the DLP.

230 !

240 OUTPUT OSa*,"E-XMPLE.,"             ! Have the analyzer execute the

250                                     ! DLP E,XMPLE.

260 SEND 7;UNL TALK 18 LISTEN 1 DATA ! Send to HP-IB select code 7 the

270                                     ! commands necessary-to allow

280                                     ! the analyzer (address 18) to

290                                     ! talk to the printer (address 1)

300                                     ! while this controller is still

310                                     ! connected.

320 LOCAL 7                             ! Local HP-IB select code 7.

330 WAIT 2                              ! Wait for printer to finish.

340 REMOTE 7                            ! Toggle the HP-IB REN line.

350 LOCAL @Sa                           ! Local the SA.

360 !

370 ASSIGN QSa TO *                     ! Close I/O path.

380 END

                 Date Mode
                       Syntax

                Description Used to set European (DDMMYY) or United States (MMDDYY) date

4.62 Programming
DISPOSE                                                                                   DISPOSE

         Dispose                                       user-defined
          Syntax                             L d o w n loadable,

                                                            program
                                             4 user-defined J

                                                           variable
                                                       user-defined

                                                          limit line

         Description  Dispose allows the user to free module RAM previously allocated for
                      user-defined functions. These functions include traces, user-defined
                      variables, DLPs, and limit-lines. The DISPOSE command will search
                      for the first occurrence of the indicated Ele name and delete it. The
                      command has a file search hierarchy; it searches for different types of
                      files in the order shown in Table 4-3. This table also shows examples
                      of using the DISPOSE command for each file type.

                                                         `I&ble 4-3.
                      Dispose Command File Search Hierarchy and Programming

                                                         Examples

             File                 File       Programming
         Hierarchy                5Pe           Example

         1.           Trace                  DISPOSE TRACE.961225101522.TRC

         2.           User-Defined Variable  DISPOSE D-DATA.VAR

         3.           DLP (DownLoadable Program) DISPOSE F-PRCTAM.DLP

         4.           Limit Line             DISPOSE BCDMA.LIM

                      File extensions such as .TRC, .VAR, and .DLP shown in the
                      programming examples in Table 4-3 are not required, as long as
                      the file name is unique. However, using extensions help reduce
                      the possibility that an incorrect file type is deleted. The DISPOSE
                      command chooses the first filename match in its hierarchy and ignores
                      the extension if the extension is omitted in the DISPOSE command.

                                             Programming 4-63
DISPOSE

                  A timedate stamp is included as part of the filename for a trace. If
                  there are multiple traces with the same filename, the timedate stamp
                  can be used to identify each trace individually. When the DISPOSE
                  command is used to dispose of a trace, the timedate stamp must be
                  included with the trace filename or it will dispose of the first trace
                  that it finds with that filename. The CATALOG command can be used
                  to obtain timedate stamps for traces.

                  The dispose command file search was limited to DLP files in earlier
                  revisions of firmware.

                  The DISPOSE ALL command deletes all existing files and makes
                  available the maximum amount of memory.

4-64 Programming
                                                                      DISPOSE

Program Example

10 ! The following example shows the use of the DISPOSE command. First it

20 ! clears memory. Then it creates a DLP and a variable. Finally, it deletes

30 ! the variable. Since this example disposes EVERYTHING currently downloaded

40 ! into the memory module, it should be used with caution.

50 !

60 ASSIGN QSa TO 718                    ! Assign I/O path to address 718.

70 !

80 OUTPUT QSa;"IP;DISPOSE ALL;";

90 PRINT "DISPOSE ALL executed. Press (LCL) [MODULE] (UTILITY) to"

100 PRINT "observe that there are no applicable entries."

110 DISP "Press CCONTINUEI on computer when ready"

120 PAUSE

130 !

140 OUTPUT dSa;"FUNCDEF TST,O;";        ! Create a DLP function.

150 OUTPUT OSa;"VARDEF NUMBER,999;"; ! Create, initialize a real variable.

160 OUTPUT @Sa;"ADD NUMBER,NUMBER,l;";

170 OUTPUT @Sa;"Q;";

180 !

190 OUTPUT OSa;"IP;TST;";               ! Execute the DLP creating.

200 PRINT "Press (LCL) [MODULE] (UTILITY) to observe that there are"

210 PRINT "now two entries."

220 DISP "Press [CONTINUE] on computer when ready"

230 PAUSE

240 !

250 OUTPUT QSa;"IP;DISPOSE NUMBER;";

260 PRINT "Press (LCL) [MODULE] (UTILITY) to observe that there is"

270 PRINT "now ONE entry. The variable NUMBER has been disposed."

280 !

290 ASSIGN (OSa TO *                    ! Close I/O path.

300 END

DIV

                      Divide
                      Syntax

                              i, u s e r - d e f i n e d J
                                               trace

                              Luser-defined -,
                                           variable

                              L predef ined ,
                                           variable

                              re:predefined           4 user-defined
                                            function                   trace
                                        trace range
                                              number  4 user-defined A
                                                                   variable

                                                                   variable

Description                   Divides source 1 by source 2, then stores the results in the
                              destination. A divide-by-zero attempt generates an error, attaches the

                              sends it to the destination.

4-66 Programming
                                                                        DIV

Program Example

10 ! The following example shows the use of the DIV command. The example

20 ! uses an input signal of 300MHz, such as the CAL OUTPUT.

30 ! An input signal is not necessary to the function of

40 ! this example, but acts as a visual aid.

50 !

60 ASSIGN QSa TO 718              ! Assign I/O path to address 718.

70 !

80 OUTPUT QSa;"FUNCDEF DIVEX,Q;"; ! Logical start of the DLP function.

100 OUTPUT OSa;"TRDEF ARY,300;"; ! Create 300 element trace.

110 OUTPUT QSa;"IP; CF 300MHZ;SP IMHZ;";

                                  ! Instrument preset; set center

120                               ! frequency and span.

130 OUTPUT (OSa;"CLRW TRA; TS;"; ! Clear trace A and take a sweep.

140 OUTPUT @Sa;"VIEW TRA;";       ! View trace A.
150 OUTPUT QISa; "MOV ARY,TRA;";  ! Move first 300 points of trace A

160                               ! into user-defined trace.

170 OUTPUT (OSa;"DIV TRA,ARY,2;"; ! Divide user-defined trace by 2 and

180                               ! place result in trace A.

190 OUTPUT @Sa;"VIEW TRA;";       ! View result.

200 OUTPUT (OSa;"Q;";             ! Logical end of DLP function.

210 !

220 OUTPUT QSa;"DIVEX;";          ! Execute function.

230 !

240 ASSIGN (DSa TO *              ! Close I/O path.

250 END

     Note        Since in this example the 300-point user-defined trace called ARY is
                 smaller than the 600-point trace A, the last value in the user-defined
                 trace is used as an operand on the remaining elements of trace A.

                                                          Programming 4.67
DSPLY                         rdisploy v a r i a b l e ,

        Display Variable
                      Syntax

Description                   Displays the value of the variable at the current position of the
    Note                      graphics pen. Refer to the OR, PA, PR, PU, or PD commands. The
                              variable is displayed according to the field-width specifier and the
                              decimal places assigned to the current position. Field width is made
                              up of all digits, including the sign and decimal point. If the variable
                              value is too large to fit into the field-width and decimal-places
                              specification, exponential notation is used.

                              For the OR command to function properly in conjunction with the
                              DSPLY command, you must use PU or PA after OR to position the
                              graphics pen correctly.

                              Prerequisite Command: LCLVAR or VARDEF when using a
                              user-defined variable, user-defined trace or user-defined array.

                              Related Commands: CLRDSP, PA, and PR

                              Field Width: 1 to 16

                              Decimal-Place Range: Minimum = 0. Maximum = if Field Width is
                              >3, then maximum is Field Width -3, otherwise it is 0.

4-68 Programming
Program Example

10 ! The following example shows the use of the DSPLY command. The example

20 ! uses values from HPBASIC and concatenates them into the statements as

30 ! they are sent to the spectrum analyzer.

40 !

50 ASSIGN 8Sa TO 718                   ! Assign I/O path to address 718.

60 !                                   ! Clears the analyzer display.
70 OUTPUT QSa;"IP;CLRDSP;";

80 !                                   ! \ Initialize variables.
90 OUTPUT QSa;"VARDEF LOG,TEN,O;";

100 OUTPUT QSa;"VARDEF YVAL,700";      !/

110 !

120 FOR I=100 TO 1000 STEP 100         ! Begin loop.

130      OUTPUT @Sa;"LOG LOG-TEN,"BtVAL$(I)&",l;";

                                       ! Take the LOG and multiply

140                                    ! by scaling factor of 1.

150      OUTPUT QSa;"PU;PA 20,YVAL;";  ! Position "pen" for text.

160      OUTPUT (PSa;"TEXT #The LOG of %;"; ! Write text.

170      OUTPUT OSa;"DSPLY "&VAL$(I)&",6,3;";

                                       ! Write a value.

180      OUTPUT (DSa;"TEXT % = %;'I;   ! Write text.

190      OUTPUT OSa;"DSPLY LOG,TEN,5,3;"; ! Write a value.

200      OUTPUT QSa;"SUB YVAL,YVAL,SO;"; ! Calculate new "pen" position.

210 NEXT I                             ! End loop.

220 !

230 ASSIGN QlSa TO *                   ! Close I/O path.

240 END

130 ! This program demonstrates the use of the EDITDONE command

240 OUTPUT 718;"LIMTFL;";        ! Set type of segment to flat.

270 OUTPUT 718;"LIMF 290MHZ;";   ! Set the frequency of segment 2.

280 OUTPUT 718;"LIMU -35DBM;LIML -8ODBM;";

290 OUTPUT 718;"LIMTSL;";        ! Set amplitude data for segment.
                                 ! Set segment type to slope.

                                 ! Set up analyzer to display

                                                                Programming 4-7 1
EDITLIML

          Edit Limit Line
                      Syntax

Description                   This command turns off the currently active limit line, then places you
                              in limit-line edit mode. Use this command with the commands SEDI
                              and SADD to call up a limit-line segment for editing. The editing of
                              each segment is terminated with SDON. EDITLIML is terminated with
                              the EDITDONE command.

                              Related commands are SENTER and SEDI.

4-74 Programming
                                                                                                 ENTER

ENTER                         ,-HP-IB a d d r e s s - ,
                                        user-defined
       Enter from HP-IB                      variable
                      Syntax
                                          predefined J

Description                   This command allows a DLP, or user-defined function, to enter
                              data over HP-IB. If the spectrum analyzer is not the controller, the
                              command aborts. The controller that is active and detected on the
                              bus has control. Use the CTRLHPIB command to gain control of the
                              HP-IB, then RELHPIB after the ENTER command to relinquish HP-IB
                              control. The data entered is formatted as specified by the format field
                              of the syntax diagram. This format may be one of the following:

                              n K, a free field (ASCII real-number format)
                              n B, one binary byte
                              n W, one binary word (two bytes)

                              user-defined variable, user-defined trace, or user-defined array.

                                                               Programming 4-77
FUNCDEF

    Function Definition
                      Syntax

Description                   This command allows you to define a program which is identified as
                              a user-defined function or a DLl? If the user-defined function label is
                              the same as a command mnemonic, an error results and the command
                              is ignored.

                              This command requires user memory in the module to execute.
                              Memory is allocated by executing this function and becomes free user
                              memory with the DISPOSE command.

                              Restrictions: Subject to available memory. The following commands
                              consume memory: ONEOS, ARRAYDEF, TRDEF, KEYDEF, LCLVAR,
                              VARDEF REPEAT/UNTIL, and IF/THEN.

                              Related Commands: ABORT, RETURN, ERASE, and DISPOSE

4-76 Programming
                                                                       FUNCDEF

Program Example

10 ! The following example shows the use of the FUNCDEF command. The example

20 ! uses an input signal of 300 MHz, such as the CAL OUTPUT, and looks at

30 ! its harmonics. An input signal is not necessary to the function of

40 ! this example, but acts as a visual aid.

50 !

60 ASSIGN (PSa TO 718                     ! Assign I/O path to address 718.

70 !

80 OUTPUT QSa;"FUNCDEF INCRFREQ,O;"; ! Logical start of the DLP function.

90 OUTPUT QSa;"VARDEF FREQ,300E6;"; ! Create variable and initialize

                                          ! to 300 MHz.

100 OUTPUT QSa;"IP;SP IMHZ;";     ! Instrument preset.

110 !

120 OUTPUT OSa;"REPEAT;";                 ! Begin loop.

130 OUTPUT (QSa;" MOV CF,FRED;";          ! Set center frequency.

140 OUTPUT Wa;" TS;";                     ! Take a sweep to update display.

150 OUTPUT QISa;" ADD FREQ,FREQ,300EG;";

                                          ! Increase FRED by 300 MHz.

160 OUTPUT QSa;YJNTIL FREQ,GT,3ES;"; ! End of loop.

170 OUTPUT (PSa9*"Q-t",*                  ! Logical end of DLP function.

180 !

190 !

200 OUTPUT (DSa;" INCRFREQ;";             ! Execute function.

210 !

230 END

                                                                   Programming 4-79
IF/TEIEN/ELSE/ENDIF

      If/Then/Else/Endif
                      Syntax

                                                                                                                  f

Description       The IF/THEN/ELSE/ENDIF statement combination allows the
                  comparison of two operands to a condition. If the condition is true,
                  the command list following THEN is executed. If false, commands
                  following either the next ELSE or ENDIF statements are executed.
                  Valid conditions are less than (IX), greater than (GT), less than or
                  equal to (LE), greater than or equal to (GE), equal (EQ), and not equal
                  (NE).

                  TEEN The THEN command is treated as a no-operation function,
                              but is required for user program flow purposes.

                  ELSE The ELSE command delimits the alternate condition of an
                              IF command.

                  ENDIF The ENDIF command delimits the end of a conditional
                              command sequence.

4-90 Programming
                                                        IF/THEN/ELSE/ENDIF

Program Example

10 !The following example shows the use of the IF/THEN/ELSE/ENDIF command.

20 !The instructions within the FUNCDEF '6' delimiters form a structure

30 !called a DLP (downloadable program).

40 !

50 ASSIGN @Sa TO 718             ! Assign I/O path to address 718.

60 !

70 OUTPUT QSa;"FUNCDEF TST,(B;"; ! Logical start of the DLP.

80 OUTPUT QSa;"VARDEF Y,500;";   ! Create variable and initialize to 500.

90 OUTPUT QSa;"VARDEF X,500;";   ! Create variable and initialize to 500.

100 OUTPUT QSa;"CLRDSP;";        ! Clear display.

110 OUTPUT QSa;"REPEAT;";        ! Begin loop.

120 OUTPUT QSa;" IF Y,LT,lOO; THEN;";

                                 ! Test condition.

130 OUTPUT QSa;" PU;PA 100,X;"; ! Move pen.

140 OUTPUT (OSa;" TEXT%DLP ABORTED%;";

                                 ! Print text.

150 OUTPUT (PSa;" ABORT;";

160 OUTPUT QSa;" ELSE;";

170 OUTPUT QSa;" PU;PA 100,X;"; ! Move pen.

180 OUTPUT OSa;" DSPLY Y,5,2;"; ! Display 'Y'.

190 OUTPUT (PSa;" SUB X,X,40;";  ! Decrement pen pointer.

200 OUTPUT (OSa;" SUB Y,Y,lOO;"; ! Decrement variable.

210 OUTPUT cOSa;" ENDIF;";

220 OUTPUT QSa;"UNTIL Y,ECj,-100;"; ! End loop (The abort will occur

230                              ! before this condition will be

240                              ! satisfied.

250 OUTPUT OSa;"O;"              ! Logical end of DLP.

260 !

270 OUTPUT (DSa;"TST;"           ! Execute DLP.

280 !

290 ASSIGN QSa TO *              ! Close I/O path.

300 END

INT                             INT
                              /-destination-,
                     Integer
                      Syntax

                                                        user-defined

                                               -user-defined
                                                            variable

                                               L p r e d e f ined
                                                            variable

                                               4predefined J
                                                          function
                                                         trace range J

Description                   Stores in the destination the greatest integer number which is less
                              than or equal to the real number in the source. Since traces and trace
                              ranges consist of integers only, using them as source data in the INT
                              command yields the same result as using them as source data with the
                              MOV command.

4-82 Programming

                  10 ! The following example shows the use of the KEYCLR command.

                  30 ASSIGN OSa TO 718      ! Assign I/O path to address 718.

                  40 !

                  50 OUTPUT QSa;"KEYCLR;";  ! Clear all user-defined keys.

                  60 ASSIGN (PSa TO *       ! Close I/O path.

                  70 END

4-84 Programming
                                                                                   KEYDEF

KEYDEF                        /key n u m b e r ,

User Defined Key Definition
                      Syntax

                                                                     user-defined

                              ,- k e y l a b e l \
                                                              /
                                                                        character

      Description  KEYDEF assigns a DLP (function label) to a USER KEY (key number)
                   and labels the key with the function label. Pressing the softkey
      Parameters   executes the function or DLP There are 10 softkeys available to label.
Query Response     Softkey labels may be up to two lines long containing eight characters
                   in each line.

                   Minimum: 1
                   Maximum: 10

                   The query returns the function or softkey name.

                                                                                   Programming 4-85
KEYDEF

Program Example

                  10 ! The following example shows the use of the KEYDEF command.

                  20 !

                  30 ASSIGN QSa TO 718               ! Assign I/O path to address 718.

                  40 OUTPUT OSa;"KEYDEF 2,BOX,%DRAW BOX%;";

                                                     ! Define softkey 2 to execute the

                  50                                 ! DLP BOX when key is pressed.

                  60                                 ! Give the key a label 'DRAW BOX'.

                  70 !

                  80 OUTPUT OSa;"FUNCDEF BOX,(B;"; ! Logical start of function.

                  90 OUTPUT (OSa;" IP;";             ! Instrument preset.

                  100 OUTPUT QSa;" CLRDSP;";         ! Clear the analyzer screen.

                  110 !

                  120 OUTPUT QSa;" PU;PA 300,300;";

                                                     ! Move pen to starting point.

                  130 OUTPUT (OSa;" PD;PR 240,O;"; ! \

                  140 OUTPUT cOSa " PR 0,240;";      ! \ Draw

                  150 OUTPUT OSa " PR -240,O;";      ! / Rectangle

                  160 OUTPUT OSa " PR 0,-240;";      !/

                  170 OUTPUT @Sa 'I@ ; " ;           ! Logical end of function.

                  180 !

                  190 ASSIGN QSa TO *                ! Close I/O path.

                  200 END

4-86 Programming
                              LCLVAR

LCLVAR

            Local Variable
                      Syntax

Description                   A local variable is a variable that is defined within a FUNCDEF. It
    Note                      is recognized only when the FUNCDEF which defined it is running.
                              Local variables have no meaning in a program outside the FUNCDEF
                              that defined it, but are recognized by any FUNCDEF that is called up
                              by the defining FUNCDEF.

                              In Mass Memory Modules with firmware datecode 910116 and later,
                              the value of a user-defined variable can be modified using the
                              secondary keyword EP. Refer to "Using EP to Modify User-Defined
                              Variables (firmware revision 910116 and later)" in this chapter.

                              Memory is allocated for the local-variable operation only as long as its
                              defining FUNCDEF is running. Memory used by the local variable is
                              freed at the completion of the defining FUNCDEF.

                              Programming 4-87
LCLVAR

Program Example

10 ! The following example shows the use of the LCLVAR command. This example

20 ! calculates the power in milliwatts of the largest signal on the analyzer

30 ! CRT. Initial settings are sent outside the function to set up the

40 ! CAL OUTPUT signal on the CRT. These settings act as a visual aid.
50 I

60 ASSIGN QSa TO 718                 ! Assign I/O path to address 718.

70 !

80 OUTPUT OSa;"IP; CF BOOMHZ; SP IOOMHZ;";

                                     ! CAL OUTPUT signal to center screen.

90 !

100 OUTPUTQSa;"FUNCDEFDBM,TO,MW,@;"; ! Logical start of DLP.

110 OUTPUT QSa;" LCLVAR PWR,MW,O;";

                                     ! Define local variable and

                                            initialize to 0.

120 OUTPUT (OSa;" TS;MKPK HI;";      ! Take sweep; marker to peak;

130 OUTPUT (DSa;" EXP PWR,MW,MKA,lO;"; ! pwr-mw = IO-(mka/lO)

150 OUTPUT OSa;" PU;PA 100,650;PD;"; ! Move to starting position.

160 OUTPUT (DSa;" TEXT IPOWER (mW>=%;"; ! Write text.

170 OUTPUT (OSa;" DSPLY PWR,MW,5,3;"; ! Write results.

180 OUTPUT @Sa*#"O*9",*              ! Logical end of DLP.

190 !

200 OUTPUTQSa;"DBM,TOJiW;";          ! Execute DLP.

210 !

220 ASSIGN cOSa TO *                 ! Close I/O path.

230 END

4-68 Programming
LIMD                                                                       LIMD

         Limit-Line Delta                 /amplitude,
                      Syntax

Description                   Use this command to enter the delta value for the amplitude of a
                              limit-line segment. Related commands are EDITLIML and SEDI. This
                              command is used along with LIMM to define the deviation, both
                              positive and negative, from a middle value.

Parameter Number Range: -175 dB to 50 dB

                                          Programming 4-88
LIMD

      Program Example

      120 ! This program demonstrates the use of the LIMD command

      130 ! to generate a limit line:

      150 !

      160 OUTPUT 718;"LIMIPURGE;";                      ! Purge current limit line.

      170 OUTPUT 718*"EDITLIML*"*                       ! Begin edit of limit line.

      180 OUTPUT 718;"LIMIREL ;lF;;";                   ! Make sure it's an absolute limit line.

      190 OUTPUT 718*, "SADD*,".,                       ! Add segment to the limit line.

      200 OUTPUT 718;"SEDI I;";                         ! Edit the first segment.

      210 OUTPUT 718;"LIMF 250MHZ;"; ! Set frequency of the segment.

      220 OUTPUT 718;"LIMU -35DBM;"; ! Provide the upper amplitude.

      230 OUTPUT 718;"LIML -8ODBM;"; ! Enter the lower amplitude.

      240 OUTPUT 718;"LIMTFL;";                         ! Set type of segment to flat.

      250 OUTPUT 718;"SDON;";                           ! Enter parameters into table.

      260 OUTPUT 718;"SADD;SEDI 2;";                    ! Add, then edit second segment.

      270 OUTPUT 718;"LIMF 290MHZ;";                    ! Set frequency of segment 2.

      280 OUTPUT 718;"LIMU -35DBM;LIML-80DBM;";

                                                        ! Set amplitude data for segment.

      290 OUTPUT 718;"LIMTSL;";                         ! Set segment type to slope.

      300 OUTPUT 718~"SDON~"~                           ! Enter parameters into table.

      310 OUTPUT 718;"SADD;SEDI 3;";

      320 OUTPUT 718;"LIMF 296.5MHZ;LIMU -5DBM;LIML-80DBM;LIMTSL;";

      330 OUTPUT 718*"SDON*"*

      340 OUTPUT 718;"SADD;SEDI 4;";

      350 OUTPUT 718;"LIMF 300MHZ;LIMM -15DBM;LIMD 10DBM;LIMTSL;";

      360 OUTPUT 718;"SDON;";

      370 OUTPUT 718 "SADD;SEDI 5;";

      380         OUTPUT  718  .  "LIMF  303.5MHZ;LIMU  -5DBM;LIML  -8ODBM;LIMTSL;";
                               9

      390 OUTPUT 718 "SDON;";

      400 OUTPUT 718 i "SADD;SEDI 6;";

      410 OUTPUT 718 "LIMF 304.5MHZ;LIMU -5DBM;LIML -8ODBM;LIMTSL;";

      420 OUTPUT 718 ;"SDON;";

      430 OUTPUT 718;"SADD;SEDI 7 . II .

      440 OUTPUT 718;"LIMF 310MHZ ;L;MU -35DBM;LIML -8ODBM;LIMTFL;";

      450 OUTPUT 718*"SDON*"*

      460         OUTPUT 718;"SADD;SEDI  8 . II .
                                              ,,

      470 OUTPUT 718;"LIMF 350MHZ ;LIMU -35DBM;LIML -8ODBM;LIMTFL;";

      480 OUTPUT 718;"SDON;";

      490 OUTPUT 718;"EDITDONE;";                       ! End of limit-line definition.

      500 OUTPUT 718;"CF 300MHZ;SP 50MHZ;RB 2MHZ;VB 30KHZ;TS;";

                                                        ! Set up analyzer to display the

      505                                               ! limit line.

      510 END

4-92 Programming
                                        LIMIFAIL

LIMIFAIL                      LIMIFAIL

        Limit-Fail Query
                      Syntax

Description                   This command is a query which returns a zero when a limit-line test
                              passes. If an active trace fails the lower-amplitude parameter, the
                              query response is 1; an upper-amplitude failure query response is 2. If
                              an active trace fails both upper- and lower-limits, the query response
                              is 3.

                                        Programming 4-93
LIMIPURGE

        Purge Limit Line
                      Syntax

Description                   Deletes the current limit line, but does not remove any limit-line
                              tables saved in the module RAM. Use the DISPOSE command to
                              remove limit-line tables from module memory.

                                                               Programming 4-95
LIMIRCL                                 /----- I i m i t I i n e names-,

       Recall Limit Line
                      Syntax

                              (LIYIRCL  de imiter  identifier

Description                   Limit lines stored on memory cards using a mass-memory module with
    Note                      firmware datecode 910116 or later cannot be read into a module with
                              an earlier firmware datecode (for example, 890524). Limit lines stored
                              on cards using modules with firmware datecode 890524 or earlier can
                              be read into a module with firmware datecode 910116 or later.

                              Recalls a limit-line set from the limit-line table in the module user
                              memory. The table is stored in user memory with the LIMISAVE
                              command. The command displays a limit line which is recalled by
                              the name assigned to it. A limit line may be saved and given a name
                              using the remote command LIMISAV, or entered from the front panel
                              with the screen-title function. `lb recall a limit line from a memory
                              card, use the command CARDLOAD first to copy the limit line to the
                              module memory. Use the LIMITEST ON command to display the line.

4-96 Programming
                              LIMIREL

LIMIREL

     Relative Limit line
                      Syntax

Description                   When set to ON, this command used with the EDITLIML command
                              creates a relative limit line. The default setting is OFF, which makes
                              the limit line amplitude and frequency parameters absolute.

4.98 Programming
                                                                           LIMBAV

LIMISAV                       del i m i t e r  identifier  de i m i t e r

         Limit-Line Save
                      Syntax

Description                   Limit lines stored on memory cards using a mass-memory module with
    Note                      firmware datecode 910116 or later cannot be read into a module with
                              an earlier firmware datecode (for example, 890524). Limit lines stored
                              on cards using modules with firmware datecode 890524 or earlier can
                              be read into a module with firmware datecode 910116 or later.

                              This command saves the active limit line to module memory under
                              the name assigned to it. Any previously existing limit line having the
                              same name is overwritten with the new limit-line table data. Refer
                              also to the LIMIRCL command. lb save a limit line to the memory
                              card, execute CARDSTORE after the LIMISAV command.

                                                           Programming 4-99
LIMITEST

          Limit-Line Test
                      Syntax

Description                   This command activates the limit-line test function, which compares
                              the trace data in the current sweep with the limits set up in the limit
                              table of the active limit line. The results of the current active trace
                              compared with the active limit line can be read using the LIMIFAIL
                              command. When this command is set to ON, the active limit-line test
                              limits are displayed on-screen along with a LIMIT FAILED message if
                              the trace data fails.

LIMTFL

Flat Limit-Line Segment
                      Syntax

Description This command is used within the SEDI command to make the selected

                       limit-line segment flat.

                              Programming 4-105
LIMTFL

Program Example

        130 ! This program demonstrates the use of the LIMTFL command

        140 ! to generate a limit line:

        150 !

        160 OUTPUT718;"LIMIPURGE;";      ! Purge current limit line.

        170 OUTPUT718;"EDITLIML;";       ! Begin edit of limit line.

        180 OUTPUT 718;"LIMIREL OFF;";   ! Make sure it's an absolute limit line.

        190 OUTPUT 718*,"SADD*9"*8       ! Add segment to the limit line.

        200 OUTPUT 718,*"SEDI 1.9 ".9     ! Edit the first segment.
        210 OUTPUT 718;"LIMF 250MHZ;";   ! Set frequency of the segment.

        220 OUTPUT 718;"LIMU -35DBM;"; ! Provide the upper amplitude.

        230 OUTPUT 718;"LIML -8ODBM;"; ! Enter the lower amplitude.

        240 OUTPUT718;"LIMTFL;";         Set type of segment to flat.

        250 OUTPUT 718*"SDON*"*          Enter parameters into table.

        260 OUTPUT 718;"SADD;SEDI 2;";   Add, then edit second segment.

        270 OUTPUT 718;"LIMF 290MHZ;";   ! Set frequency of segment 2.

        280 OUTPUT718;"LIMU-35DBM;LIML-8ODBM;";

                                         ! Set amplitude data for segment.

        290 OUTPUT718;"LIMTSL;";         ! Set segment type to slope.

        300 OUTPUT 718;"SDON;";          ! Enter parameters into table.

        310 OUTPUT 718;"SADD;SEDI 3;";

        320 OUTPUT 718;"LIMF 296.5MHZ;LIMW5DBM;LIML-8ODBM;LIMTSL;";

        330 OUTPUT 718;"SDON;";

        340 OUTPUT 718;"SADD;SEDI 4;";

        350 OUTPUT 718;"LIMF 300MHZ;LIMWSDBM;LIML-25DBM;LIMTSL;";

        360 OUTPUT 718~"SDON~"~

        370 OUTPUT 718;"SADD;SEDI 5;";

        380 OUTPUT 718;"LIMF 303.5MHZ;LIMW5DBM;LIML-8ODBM;LIMTSL;";

        390 OUTPUT 718;"SDON;";

        400 OUTPUT 718;"SADD;SEDI 6;";

        410 OUTPUT 718;"LIMF 304.5MHZ;LIMW5DBM;LIML-8ODBM;LIMTSL;";

        420 OUTPUT 718*"SDON."*

        430 OUTPUT 718I"SADD;SEDI 7;";

        440 OUTPUT 718;"LIMF 3lOMHZ;LIMW35DBM ;LIML-80DBM;LIMTFL;";

        450 OUTPUT 718;"SDON;";

        460 OUTPUT 718;"SADD;SEDI 8;";

        470 OUTPUT 718;"LIMF 350MHZ;LIMW35DBM ;LIML-80DBM;LIMTFL;";

        480 OUTPUT 718;"SDON;";

        490 OUTPUT718;"EDITDONE;";       ! End of limit-line definition.

        491

LOG

     Logarithm        -destination7
         Syntax

                 LOG           TRA           /

                               TRB p

                      L user-defined J
                                       trace

                      Luser-defined -,
                                   variable

                      i, p r e d e f ined J
                                  variable

                      Ltrace range J

                 I - Source -       /scol i n g f a c t o r ,

     Description The logarithm (base 10) of the source is taken, the result is multiplied

                           by a specified scaling factor, then sent to the destination.

                                                               Programming 4-l 11
LOG

     Program Examples

     Example #l

10 ! The following example shows the use of the LOG command. The example

20 ! uses values from HP BASIC and concatenates them into the statements as

30 ! they are sent to the spectrum analyzer.

40 !

50 ASSIGN cDSa TO 718                 ! Assign I/O path to address 718.

60 !

70 OUTPUT OSa;"CLRDSP;";              ! Clears the analyzer display.

80 !

90 OUTPUT (OSa; "VARDEF LOG,TEN,O;"; ! \ Initialize variables.

100 OUTPUT OSa;"VARDEF YVAL,700;"; ! /

110 !

120 FOR I=100 TO 1000 STEP 100        ! Begin loop.

130                OUTPUT (DSa;"LOG LOG,TEN,"&VAL$(I>&",l;";

                                      ! Take the LOG and multiply

140                                   ! by scaling factor of 1.

150 OUTPUT QSa;"PU;PA 20,YVAL;"; ! Position "pen" for text.

160 OUTPUT (OSa;"TEXT %The LOG of 1;";

                                      ! Write text.

170 OUTPUT OSa;"DSPLY "%VAL$(I>&",6,3;";

                                      ! Write a value.

180 OUTPUT (OSa;"TEXT % = %;I';       ! Write text.

190 OUTPUT QSa;"DSPLY LOG_TEN,5,3;";

                                      ! Write a value.

200 OUTPUT QISa;"SUB YVAL,YVAL,50;"; ! Calculate new "pen" position.

210 NEXT I                            ! End loop.

220 !

230 ASSIGN QSa TO *                   ! Close I/O path.

240 END

4-112 Programming
                                                                               LOG

     Example #2

10 !The following example shows another use of the LOG command. This example

20 !performs the same function as the previous example, but is fully

30 !downloadable. Note that only minor changes need to be made to convert

40 !a program from computer-dependent to computer-independent! This method

50 !is also visibly much faster for the spectrum analyzer to execute.

60 !

70 ASSIGN (OSa TO 718                          ! Assign I/O path to address 718.

80 !

90 OUTPUT QSa;"FUNCDEF LOG-EX,O;";

                                               ! Logical start of DLP.

100 OUTPUT @Sa;"CLRDSP;";                      ! Clears the analyzer display.

110 !

120 OUTPUT OSa;"VARDEF LOG,TEN,O;";            ! \ Initialize variables.

130 OUTPUT OSa;"VARDEF YVAL,700;";             !/

140 OUTPUT QSa;"VARDEF LP,CTR,lOO;";           ! Initialize loop counter.

150 !

160 OUTPUT OSa;"REPEAT;";                      ! Begin loop.

170      OUTPUT (DSa;"LOG LOG,TEN,LP-CTR,l;";

                                               ! Take the LOG and multiply

175                                            ! by scaling factor of 1.

190      OUTPUT QSa;"PU;PA 20,YVAL;";          ! Position "pen" for text.

200      OUTPUT QSa;"TEXT %The LOG of )/I;"; ! Write text.

210      OUTPUT OSa;"DSPLY LP_CTR,6,3;"; ! Write a value.

220      OUTPUT OSa;"TEXT % = %;'I;            ! Write text.

230      OUTPUT QSa;"DSPLY LOG,TEN,5,3;"; ! Write a value.

240      OUTPUT @Sa;"SUB YVAL,YVAL,SO;"; ! Calculate new "pen" position.

250      OUTPUT (OSa;"ADD LP,CTR,LP-CTR,lOO;";

                                               ! Increment loop counter.

260 OUTPUT QSa;"UNTIL LP,CTR,GT,lOOO;"; ! End loop.

270 OUTPUT OSa;"(D;"

280 !

290 OUTPUT QSa;"LOG,EX;";                      ! Execute the DLP.

300 ASSIGN @Sa TO *                            ! Close I/O path.

310 END
